Steep areas                                                                                                                                                         2
                   Z-Level / Normal (1): Machining is performed in Z-levels corresponding to the vertical infeed defined on the
                   Parameters input page.

                   Parallel (2): Machining is performed parallel to the flow direction of the rest material corresponding to the
                   horizontal offset defined on the Parameters input page.

                  2                                                                                                                                     Infeed mode
           1                                                                                                                                                               The infeed mode can be defined as needed for all machining strategies.
   2                                                                                                                                                                       Smooth: The infeed between the milling paths is the shape of a HSC loop.

Normal (1): Machining is performed parallel to the surface normals.                                                                                                        Direct: The infeed motion is carried out on the shortest path in the machining feedrate (G1) on the surface.
Z-Level (2): Machining is performed parallel to the Z-level.                                                                                                               If the linear movement would cause damage to the workpiece, the system guides the tool with surface
                                                                                                                                                                           contact (no material being removed) around the workpiece.
   1                                                                 2

      1                                                                 2

Two different machining strategies are available for each of the machining modes Z-Level and Normal.
Oneway (1): Machining is always performed in the same direction.
Zigzag (2): Machining with alternating orientation.

Retract mode                                                                                                                                             4

                   The retract mode defines the Z level where the system executes horizontal infeed movements. The mode                        Roughing
                   set is valid for all machining directions (incl. approach, retract and return movement macros) and for the
                   profiles machined with them. Exception: Machining in zigzag mode without return macro.                                                In Roughing mode, rest material areas from preceding machining are machined completely or limited to
                                                                                                                                                         the cavities.
                   Clearance distance (1): all retract and infeed movements are executed via the clearance distance. Starting
                   and end positions of an infeed movement in rapid are displaced in Z direction in order to guarantee a
                   collision-free linear infeed movement. The clearance distance is added to these positions.

                   Clearance plane (2): all retract and infeed movements are executed via the clearance plane.

Multiple allowances: example of use                                                                                                                                        Tool check setup: the tool check settings are specified in this dialogue box. For further information, see
                                                                                                                                                                           section Tool check setup (page 768)

                                                                                                                                                        Calculate length
                                                                                                                                                                           The Reduce and Extend options are available if a holder, extension or spindle is defined. Only the Reduce
                                                                                                                                                                           option is available for all 5X cycles.

                                                                                                                                                                          Extend (1): if there is a tool extension, holder or spindle collision, hyperMILL will calculate a larger tool
                                                                                                                                                                           reach, based on the tool reach defined for the tool. So there is no limit to the maximum tool reach ­ the
                                                                                                                                                                           maximum possible tool reach of a real tool is simply ignored! This is the preferred option if the selected
                                                                                                                                                                           tool is available in different lengths (e.g., short, long, extra long) and the length calculation has not yet
                                                                                                                                                                           determined the suitable real tool for the assembly. In this case, the tool is merely a geometry description
                                                                                                                                                                           with an unlimited shank length.

                                                                                                                                                                          Reduce (2): hyperMILL calculates the shortest possible collision-free tool reach, based on the defined tool
                                                                                                                                                                           reach of the tool. This is the preferred option if you only want to calculate tool lengths that are also actually
                                                                                                                                                                           possible for the tool selected in the assembly. In this assembly, you must select the maximum tool reach
                                                                                                                                                                           as the tool reach.

                                                                                                                                                        11       22

   (1) Manual limitation of the machining area (1).
   (2) Multiple allowances: Group 0: allowance = 0 (A), Group 1: allowance = -1.5 (B).
   (3) Additional surfaces: Hub surface defined as additional surface. This prevents the tool being generated
   below the bottom plane (3).

                   Check on: You must always enable this option if you want the tool to be checked for collisions. If the tool                                                      shank.
                   check is not enabled, the tool used is shown in red in the graphic preview. When this check is enabled,

                                                                              948                                                                       Retract mode

                                                                                                                                             hyperMILL                     The retract mode defines the Z level where the system executes horizontal infeed movements. The mode

Check tool                                                                                                                                                                                                 3

                   The tool check ensures that all of the components defined for the tool are protected from a possible
                   collision with the CAD model on the basis of existing material. The tool check is only available if you have
                   defined a tool and also a model to be checked.

             Check on: You must always enable this option if you want the tool to be checked for collisions. If the tool            1                                                                         44
             check is not enabled, the tool used is shown in red in the graphic preview. When this check is enabled,
             the defined clearance is used to check all of the components defined for the tool. We recommend that you
             define the clearance so that it gets continuously larger, starting with the thick shank in the direction of the
             spindle.

             Tool check setup: the tool check settings are specified in this dialogue box. For further information, see
             section Tool check setup (page 768)

For unresolvable collision
                   Unresolvable collisions may occur if:

             · It is not possible to avoid a collision in 5X machining as a result of a change to the tool orientation.                                        22

             · In 3D machining, it is not possible to resolve a collision involving the tool extension, holder and/or               Cutting edge
                spindle by extending the tool (see calculating the tool length) or a collision will occur with the thick
                shank.                                                                                                                                 3D machining of cutting edges. The cutting edge can be defined via a curve or surface. The axial
                                                                                                                                                       machining area is defined by a second curve or depth. The contour can be machined with a plunge (1) or
             If a collision cannot be avoided by changing the tool orientation (5X machining) or using a tool length                                   swarf (2) strategy. Automatic rest machining is possible by means of a reference cycle.
             calculation, the Stop option can be used to cancel the toolpath calculation when a collision occurs.

Retract mode
                   The retract mode defines the Z level where the system executes horizontal infeed movements. The mode                                         2                                             B
                   set is valid for all machining directions (incl. approach, retract and return movement macros) and for the
                   profiles machined with them. Exception: Machining in zigzag mode without return macro.
                   Clearance distance (1): all retract and infeed movements are executed via the clearance distance. Starting
                   and end positions of an infeed movement in rapid are displaced in Z direction in order to guarantee a
                   collision-free linear infeed movement. The clearance distance is added to these positions.

                   Machining of the defined area until the tool axis is on top of the boundary (1) . The tool axis can never
                   move beyond the boundary.                                                                                                            Plunge macro
                   Offset: A positive value enlarges the boundary, while a negative value reduces it.
                                                                                                                                                                           Various plunge strategies for infeed movement to the next machining level are available for all level-by-lev-
                                1                                                                                                                                          el machining cycles (except Z Level Finishing).
                                                                                                                                                                           Axial (A): The infeed is executed as a linear movement in Z direction.
                                                                                                                                                                           Use the axial plunge macro for:

                                                                                                                                                                                 · Infeed without stock removal (user-defined plunge point is outside of stock or boundary).
                                                                                                                                                                                 · Pre-drilling at the existing user-defined plunge point.
                                                                                                                                                                                 · Axial plunging with a tool suitable for vertical chip removal.

                                                                                                                                                                Helical (B): used to remove the stock of 3D pockets. Helix radius (1): offset of the cutter centre point
                                                                                                                                                                relative to the axis of the helix (= Z axis through plunge point). Angle (2): lead angle of the helix. The
                                                                                                                                                                number of revolutions executed by the tool in the course of the infeed movement is computed from the
                                                                                                                                                                value of the vertical stepdown and the lead angle of the helix. The helix direction is defined by the selected
                                                                                                                                                                orientation (climb/conventional milling).

                                                                                                                                                                Ramp (C): ramping-in proceeds along the first milling path. Angle (3): enter the lead angle of the ramp.

Retract macros are additional motions during retraction after the last milling path of a cycle, retraction for
horizontal stepover between the milling paths via the clearance plane or clearance distance. The following
approach macros are available depending on the machining cycle.

Axial (1): Linear movement along the tool axis towards or away from the part. Enter length of movement.
Closed and concave (inner) contours.

Circular (2): Approach and retract movements are performed in a quarter circle. Enter radius of the circle.

                                                                                                                                                                           XY.

                                                                                                                                                                           Shape Offset Finishing. The transition to the macro movement takes place without abruptly changing
                                                                                                                                                                           direction and without slowing down the machining speed. Visible machining marks are avoided.

Max. compensation value: The value should be no more than 10% of the cutter diameter. If the parameter
is set to 0.1, the machining tool must be no more than 0.1 mm larger than the tool programmed in
hyperMILL.

The 3D path compensation function is available only with a specially adjusted postpro-
cessor. Without this adjustment, the NC program cannot correct this output and, as
a result, both the component and machine may be damaged if the milling geometry
used differs from the milling geometry used to calculate the toolpath. To adjust your
postprocessor, please contact your OPEN MIND partner.

                   Min. rest material height (1): Areas with a smaller rest material height are not machined.
                   Resolution value: Accuracy for determining the rest material areas. Values between 0.25 and 0.5 are
                   recommended.

                                                                                                                                                        Pencil Milling

                                                                                                                                                                           Automatically recognise grooves of a model and generate toolpaths running parallel to the contour for
                                                                                                                                                                           each groove. The grooves can be cut free by a rough cut with a reference tool, in order to guarantee for
                                                                                                                                                                           the following machining of the adjacent surfaces an accurate approach and retract behaviour of the tool.
                                                                                                                                                                           The Pencil Milling cycle is mainly used as a preparation for high-speed milling.

                   Tapered tools may be used, but the conical shape is not used in the toolpath collision avoidance calcula-

Reference tool
                   The reference tool is used to calculate the grooves. It is of the same type as the machining tool. The
                   reference tool is defined based on the diameter. As a rule of thumb: You want the diameter of the
                   reference tool to be up to 20% greater than the diameter of the machining tool.

                                                                                                                                                                       1                                     1

              The reference diameter should be selected so that the reference tool can no longer                                                                    Flat areas (1): Only flat areas (surface slope < defined slope angle) are machined with climb or conven-
              completely remove the stock of the groove. In other words, there should always be two                                                                 tional milling.
              contact points with the surface so that the groove can be recognised.
                                                                                                                                                                    Steep only (2): Only steep areas (surface slope > defined slope angle) are machined with plunging.

                                                                                                                                                                    All areas (3): Flat areas are machined with climb or conventional milling, steep areas are machined with
                                                                                                                                                                    plunging.

                   The tool check ensures that all of the components defined for the tool are protected from a possible
                   collision with the CAD model on the basis of existing material. The tool check is only available if you have                                   1
                   defined a tool and also a model to be checked.
                                                                                                                                                                                                                          1003
                   Check on: You must always enable this option if you want the tool to be checked for collisions. If the tool
                   check is not enabled, the tool used is shown in red in the graphic preview. When this check is enabled,
                   the defined clearance is used to check all of the components defined for the tool. We recommend that you

For unresolvable collision
                   Unresolvable collisions may occur if:

                         · It is not possible to avoid a collision in 5X machining as a result of a change to the tool orientation.

                         · In 3D machining, it is not possible to resolve a collision involving the tool extension, holder and/or
                            spindle by extending the tool (see calculating the tool length) or a collision will occur with the thick
                            shank.

       If a collision cannot be avoided by changing the tool orientation (5X) or tool length calculation, various
       options are available for the machining strategies:

       Stop: The toolpath calculation stops when a collision occurs (applies for all machining strategies).

       Clip: The toolpath is fully calculated. Only the collision-free areas of the toolpaths are output. As a result of
       rework machining with another tool and/or another tool inclination, the toolpaths found in the collision can
       be tracked and checked for collisions (applies to all 3D machining strategies).

                                                                                  hyperMILL

12. 5X Machining

General information

                   The basic settings in the cycles shown in this chapter apply to all 5X cycles. Information that applies to
                   all cycles of a package is summarised at the beginning of the respective chapter. Special parameters and
                   settings that only apply to a specific cycle are dealt with in subsequent chapters.

Terms

       1. Rotary axis: Rotation around Z corresponds to the C axis.

       2. Rotary axis: Inclination to Z normally corresponds to the A or B axis.

Setup

Check tool
                   In addition to the known factors, the complete definition of the tools and tool holder forms the basis for
                   proper collision monitoring during 5X Machining.

                  3
                       4

                   Check on: When the collision check is activated, the toolpath is only calculated and executed up to the
                   collision situation. As the collision check can only check against model geometry and not the existing
                   material, the tool holder dimensions should be assigned generously.

                   Collision avoidance

                   If a potential collision of the tool tip or tool holder is determined, the system modifies the defined tilt angle
                   accordingly.

                   If there is no possible tilt angle that prevents a collision, the toolpath is only calculated and executed up to
                   the collision situation.

NC parameters
                   Max. angle increment: The maximum angle increment restricts the permissible change of the tool inclina-
                   tion between two points.
